r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given N strings S_1,S_2,\ldots,S_N. Each string consists of lowercase English letters.
For each k=1,2,\ldots,N, solve the following problem.

Let T=S_k and consider performing the following two types of operations any number of times in any order:

- Pay a cost of 1 to delete the last character of T. This operation is possible when T is not empty.
- Pay a cost of 1 to add any lowercase English letter to the end of T.

Find the minimum total cost needed to make T either empty or match one of S_1,S_2,\ldots,S_{k-1}.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
S_1
S_2
\vdots
S_N

Output

Print N lines.
The i-th line (1\le i\le N) should contain the answer for k=i.

Constraints


- 1\le N\le 2\times 10^5
- Each S_i is a string of length at least 1 consisting of lowercase English letters.
- \displaystyle \sum_{i=1}^N |S_i|\le 2\times 10^5

Sample Input 1

3
snuke
snuki
snuuk

Sample Output 1

5
2
4

For k=1, you can make T empty by performing the delete operation five times.
For k=2, you can make T match S_1 by deleting the last character and then adding e to the end.
For k=3, you can make T match S_2 by deleting the last character twice, then adding k to the end, and finally adding i to the end.

Sample Input 2

3
abc
arc
agc

Sample Output 2

3
3
3

Sample Input 3

8
at
atatat
attat
aatatatt
attattat
ttatta
tta
tt

Sample Output 3

2
4
3
8
3
6
3
1

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

def insert(s):
    v=0
    L=len(s)
    for i,char in enumerate(s):
        c=ord(char)-97
        # before moving? Need update node v with source child? Actually for current prefix node v, string s is in subtree(v) via child c. So update best at v with source child (the child after moving). We need know child node. If child doesn't exist create.
        if c in ch[v]:
            u=ch[v][c]
        else:
            u=len(ch); ch[v][c]=u; append...
        update(v, L, u)  # source child u
        v=u
    # after loop, v terminal. update(v, L, TERMINAL_SRC)
    update(v, L, -1)
```

Now, query algorithm:
```

    v=0
    best=0
    L=len(s)
    for i,char in enumerate(s):
        c=...
        u = ch[v].get(c)
        # candidate at node v excluding child u if u exists, else excluding none? If u doesn't exist, then no strings continue along q from v; all strings in subtree(v) have lcp exactly depth(v). Let's write function:
```
def add_candidate(v, length, src):
    l1 = best1_len[v]; s1 = best1_src[v]
    l2 = best2_len[v]; s2 = best2_src[v]
    if src == s1:
        if length < l1:
            best1_len[v] = length
        return
    if src == s2:
        if length < l2:
            best2_len[v] = length
            if length < l1:
                # swap best1 and best2
                best1_len[v], best2_len[v] = best2_len[v], best1_len[v]
                best1_src[v], best2_src[v] = best2_src[v], best1_src[v]
        return
    # new source
    if length < l1:
        best2_len[v] = l1; best2_src[v] = s1
        best1_len[v] = length; best1_src[v] = src
    elif length < l2:
        best2_len[v] = length; best2_src[v] = src
```
Need ensure if l1 INF and s1 -1, l2 INF. New source length < INF -> best1 set, best2 old INF. Good.

If src == s1 and length < l1, no swap needed because best1 remains min. If length > l1 no change. If src==s2 and length > l2 no change. If src==s2 and length == l2? no change. Now, one subtlety: We update node v with source child u for every prefix. But for a node v, the "branch" for a string s is the child node u along s. If two different strings pass through same child u, they are same branch. The minimum length for branch u is min length among all strings in subtree(u). We update with every string length L. add_candidate maintains top2 over branches, but if branch u's current minimum is already represented in top2, and we call with larger L, no change. If branch u's current minimum is not in top2, and we call with larger L, no change. If branch u's current minimum is in top2 but as best2, and we call with larger L, no change. If branch u's current minimum is in top2 and we call with smaller L, update. Good.

But what if branch u's current minimum is not in top2, but later best1 or best2 source? Sources never removed, so if branch u not top2 now, it will never be needed later because top2 lengths only decrease. However, wait: top2 lengths only decrease, but if branch u's min is 100, best2=50. Later best2 decreases to 40, still 100 not needed. If branch u's min is 45, it would be top2 now (since best2=50). So okay. If branch u's min is 45 but we haven't called with 45 yet? We call for every string; when the string with length 45 inserted, add_candidate with 45 will make it top2 if 45 < best2. So yes.

Now, code structure:

```
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    if not data: return
    N = int(data[0])
    strings = data[1:1+N]

    INF = 10**9
    children = [{}]
    best1_len = [INF]
    best1_src = [-1]
    best2_len = [INF]
    best2_src = [-1]

    def add_candidate(v, length, src):
        l1 = best1_len[v]
        s1 = best1_src[v]
        l2 = best2_len[v]
        s2 = best2_src[v]
        if src == s1:
            if length < l1:
                best1_len[v] = length
            return
        if src == s2:
            if length < l2:
                best2_len[v] = length
                if length < l1:
                    best1_len[v], best2_len[v] = best2_len[v], best1_len[v]
                    best1_src[v], best2_src[v] = best2_src[v], best1_src[v]
            return
        if length < l1:
            best2_len[v] = l1
            best2_src[v] = s1
            best1_len[v] = length
            best1_src[v] = src
        elif length < l2:
            best2_len[v] = length
            best2_src[v] = src

    out = []
    for s in strings:
        L = len(s)
        v = 0
        best = 0
        d = 0
        # query
        for b in s:
            c = b - 97
            u = children[v].get(c)
            if u is None:
                # no string continues; all strings in subtree(v) have lcp d
                cand_len = best1_len[v]
                if cand_len < INF:
                    val = 2 * d - cand_len
                    if val > best:
                        best = val
                break
            # candidate at v excluding child u
            if best1_src[v] != u:
                cand_len = best1_len[v]
            else:
                cand_len = best2_len[v]
            if cand_len < INF:
                val = 2 * d - cand_len
                if val > best:
                    best = val
            v = u
            d += 1
        else:
            # consumed all chars
            cand_len = best1_len[v]
            if cand_len < INF:
                val = 2 * L - cand_len
                if val > best:
                    best = val
        ans = L - best  # best >=0
        out.append(str(ans))

        # insert s
        v = 0
        for b in s:
            c = b - 97
            u = children[v].get(c)
            if u is None:
                u = len(children)
                children[v][c] = u
                children.append({})
                best1_len.append(INF)
                best1_src.append(-1)
                best2_len.append(INF)
                best2_src.append(-1)
            add_candidate(v, L, u)
            v = u
        add_candidate(v, L, -1)
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
```

Need check query `for b in s` uses bytes iteration; after break, `else` not executed. If break due to missing child, we evaluated candidate at current node v. But what if missing child at root (v=0,d=0), cand_len=best1_len[0] maybe INF if no previous; val negative; best0. ans=L. Good.
But if missing child at node v, we evaluate all subtree. However, what if there are strings in subtree(v) with lcp exactly d, but some strings in subtree(v) are in child u? There is no child u because missing, so all strings in subtree(v) have lcp exactly d. Good.
But wait, if child missing, we break without moving. We don't need to consider deeper nodes because none exist along query path. Good.

Now, one subtle bug: In query, when child exists, we evaluate candidate at v excluding child u. But what if best1_src[v] == u and best2_len[v] is INF, cand_len INF skip. Then move. Good. But what if best1_src[v] != u, cand_len=best1_len[v]. This could be a string in child w != u, lcp exactly d. But what if best1_len[v] is from terminal source -1? terminal source != u, lcp exactly d. Good.
Now, after moving to child u, d increments. At final after loop, evaluate all subtree at node v. But if query path ended because we consumed all chars, any string in subtree(v) has lcp exactly L? Wait if a string in subtree(v) is longer than query, it has prefix v (query) so lcp = L. If a string in subtree(v) is exactly v, lcp = L. If a string in subtree(v) is shorter? Cannot be in subtree(v) if shorter than L? Subtree(v) contains strings having prefix v, so length >= L. Good. So final candidate all subtree correct.
Now, what if query path ended at node v, but there are strings in subtree(v) that diverged earlier? No, if they are in subtree(v), they share entire query prefix. Good.

Now, let's think if there is a more memory-efficient children representation using arrays and linked list, and whether we should implement to avoid dict memory. The problem likely from AtCoder? N 2e5, total length 2e5. Python with dict-of-dicts accepted? Let's evaluate memory more concretely.

AtCoder Python memory limit often 1024 MB for recent, 256 MB for older. If 256 MB, list of dicts with 200k dicts might be ~50MB, arrays ~30MB, strings ~? data split stores all bytes tokens: each bytes object overhead ~33 bytes + length, 200k*~40=8MB plus list pointers 1.6MB. Total maybe <100MB. Fine. If 128 MB, maybe close but still likely under. Let's estimate:
- data = read().split(): list of N+1 bytes objects. Each bytes object overhead 33 bytes + data rounded. Average length maybe 1 if many length1, but total length 2e5, N=2e5, average 1. Each bytes object ~34 bytes, 200k*34=6.8MB, list pointers 1.6MB, total 8.4MB. If average length 10, N=20k, overhead 20k*43=0.86MB. Worst memory for bytes objects is many short strings: N=2e5, total length=2e5, so overhead ~6.8MB. Fine.
- children list of dicts: 200k dicts. Empty dict 64 bytes, but after adding one entry, dict size may be 224 bytes. Since total nodes 200k, edges 200k, each node except root has parent edge. Many nodes have one child? Actually each node's dict stores outgoing edges. Number of dicts with at least one entry equals number of nodes that have at least one child. In a trie with 200k nodes, many internal nodes have one child; leaves have empty dict. Number of internal nodes <= total nodes. Worst-case all nodes have one child (a single long string): 200k dicts, 199999 have one entry, 1 empty. Dict with 1 entry size? In CPython 3.11, empty dict 64 bytes; dict with 1 entry maybe 224 bytes? Let's check: PyDictObject includes table; minimum table size 8 entries? Actually dict starts with 8 slots? Empty dict has no table? A dict with 1 entry uses 224 bytes. So 200k*224 = 44.8MB. If branching, some dicts have multiple entries but fewer dicts? Total nodes same, internal nodes maybe less? Actually total nodes includes leaves; number of dicts with entries = internal nodes. In a trie with total nodes M, internal nodes can be M-1 in a chain. So worst 44.8MB.
- best arrays: 4 lists of length M. Each list pointers 8*M=1.6MB, total 6.4MB. Int objects: best1_len initially all INF reference same int; but after updates, many entries assigned L ints. In a chain inserting one long string, best1_len for all nodes assigned same L? Wait insert one string length 200k. For each prefix node, add_candidate(v, L, u) sets best1_len[v]=L. All entries reference same int object `L`? In Python, `L` is a local int object; assigning to many list entries stores reference to same object. So only one int object for L? Actually `L` variable is an int object; all assignments `best1_len[v] = length` store reference to that object. So no new int per assignment. For multiple strings, each string's L int object may be referenced by nodes where it is min. Number of distinct L int objects at most number of strings (200k) plus INF. best1_src entries reference child node index int objects from dict values. For chain, each node's best1_src assigned `u` int object from `len(children)` or dict value. `u = len(children)` creates int object; stored in dict and best_src. Now, let's consider if there is a hidden issue with the problem statement: "For each k=1..N, let T=S_k and consider performing operations any number of times in any order. Find minimum total cost needed to make T either empty or match one of S_1..S_{k-1}." We assumed target can be empty or any previous string. Operations can be interleaved, but any sequence that ends at U can be reduced to delete suffix then append suffix? Need prove: Since only delete last and append last, the set of characters that remain from original T must be a prefix of T (because deletions remove suffix, appends add after; you cannot delete appended chars? Actually you could append then delete, but that would waste cost. You could delete some, append, delete appended, etc. Optimal no need to delete appended chars because they don't help reach a final string that is a previous string/empty; any appended char that is later deleted is wasted. You could also append before deleting? Appending to end then deleting last would just waste. So optimal sequence is delete d chars then append a chars. The remaining prefix length L = |T|-d. Final U = prefix + appended suffix, so prefix must be prefix of U. Thus L<=|U| and prefix length L common prefix. Cost d+a = |T|-L + |U|-L = |T|+|U|-2L. To minimize for a given U, choose L as large as possible? Cost decreases with L, so L should be lcp(T,U). But can we choose L less than lcp? That would cost more, not optimal. So formula. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility to make T empty by deleting all chars cost |T|. We include score0. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a previous string U such that lcp(T,U)=0, cost |T|+|U| > |T|, so empty better. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a previous string U that is longer than T but shares all T, cost |U|-|T| (append). Score=2|T|-|U|. If |U| > 2|T|, score negative, empty better. Our algorithm handles.

Now, let's consider if there is a previous string U that is shorter than T but T starts with U, cost |T|-|U| (delete). Score=|U|. Always positive if U nonempty, so better than empty. Our algorithm handles.

Now, let's think about if there is a previous string U that is equal to T, cost0. Score=|T|. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a previous string U with lcp d and lenU such that score positive; answer less than lenT. Good.

No, query path from v goes to u; a string in child w has next char w, so cannot share more than d. Good.

If query ends at v, final candidate uses terminal. Good.

Any appended chars that are later deleted are wasted; any original chars after the final prefix are deleted. The final string's prefix of length L consists of original T prefix, and the rest appended. If you append before deleting, you might delete original chars after appended? Wait operations: delete last character of T. If you append chars, they become at end. You cannot delete original chars without first deleting appended chars. So any sequence that ends with a string U: The original T chars that remain must form a prefix of T (because to delete an original char at position i, you must delete all chars after it, including any appended). The appended chars that remain form a suffix after that prefix. Thus U = prefix of T + appended suffix. So formula. Good.

- If global min branch != u, global min.
- Else second min branch (by length) among branches != u. Since global min branch is u, second global min among all branches is necessarily from a branch != u (because branches distinct). Thus top2 global min lengths with distinct branches suffice. We maintain top2 global min lengths with distinct branches. Good.

No, branch u's min is the global min; any other string in same branch has length >= global min, so cannot be second global min unless equal? If there are two strings in same branch with same min length, we don't store a second source from same branch. Good. If global min branch u and another string in same branch also has same min length, excluding u should not use it. We don't. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the code if `best1_src[v]` and `best2_src[v]` are not updated in swap due to tuple assignment order? We did separate assignments. Good.

Let's review the swap code:
```
if length < l1:
    best1_len[v], best2_len[v] = best2_len[v], best1_len[v]
    best1_src[v], best2_src[v] = best2_src[v], best1_src[v]
```
At this point best2_len[v] has been set to `length`, best1_len[v] is l1. Tuple assignment evaluates RHS first: (best2_len[v], best1_len[v]) = (length, l1). Then assigns best1_len=length, best2_len=l1. Correct. For src: best2_src[v] is `src` (because src==s2 and we didn't change), best1_src[v] is s1. Tuple assignment RHS (src, s1), assigns best1_src=src, best2_src=s1. Correct.
If `length < l1` and we swap, but what if `best2_src[v]` was not `src`? It is because src==s2. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the code if `src == s2` and `length < l2` but `length < l1` and `s1` is -1 INF? We argued impossible if src==s2 and s1=-1 INF? Let's see state: b1=INF,s1=-1,b2=INF,s2=-1. If src=-1, first branch src==s1, not second. If src child, src != s2. So no. If b1 finite, s1 child, b2 INF,s2=-1, src=-1 terminal, length<l1 maybe, swap. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the code if `src == s1` and `length < l1`, but `best2_src[v]` is also `src` with INF? If best2_src=-1 INF and src=-1, first branch. No issue. If best2_src=src finite impossible. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the code if `length` is equal to `l1` and new source, we set best2 if length < l2. If l2 is also equal, no change. But if l2 is INF, set best2=length. Good.
If `length` is equal to `l2` and new source, no change. But if l2 is INF, length < INF true. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with the code if `length` is less than `l1` and new source, we shift old best1 to best2. But what if old best2 had same source as old best1? Not finite. If old best2 INF, okay. If old best2 finite distinct, old best1 becomes second, old best2 discarded. Correct.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with the code if `length` is less than `l1` and new source, but old best1_src is -1 INF and old best2_src is -1 INF; best2 becomes -1 INF, best1 new. Good.

New source length < terminal length. Since terminal length = depth, child length > depth, new source length could be < terminal? New source is a child branch, its length > depth, so cannot be < terminal. But if terminal length not necessarily depth? Terminal length equals depth. Child length > depth. So new child source length cannot be less than terminal. Thus if terminal exists, best1_src terminal and no new child source will replace it. But add_candidate code handles generally. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
final code.

But before final, let's ensure no syntax errors.

Code draft:

```
import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N = int(input())
    children = [{}]
    INF = 10**9
    best1_len = [INF]
    best1_src = [-1]
    best2_len = [INF]
    best2_src = [-1]

    def add_candidate(v, length, src):
        l1 = best1_len[v]
        s1 = best1_src[v]
        l2 = best2_len[v]
        s2 = best2_src[v]
        if src == s1:
            if length < l1:
                best1_len[v] = length
            return
        if src == s2:
            if length < l2:
                best2_len[v] = length
                if length < l1:
                    best1_len[v], best2_len[v] = best2_len[v], best1_len[v]
                    best1_src[v], best2_src[v] = best2_src[v], best1_src[v]
            return
        if length < l1:
            best2_len[v] = l1
            best2_src[v] = s1
            best1_len[v] = length
            best1_src[v] = src
        elif length < l2:
            best2_len[v] = length
            best2_src[v] = src

    out = []
    for _ in range(N):
        s = input().strip()
        L = len(s)

        v = 0
        best = 0
        d = 0
        for b in s:
            c = b - 97
            u = children[v].get(c)
            if u is None:
                val = 2 * d - best1_len[v]
                if val > best:
                    best = val
                break
            if best1_src[v] != u:
                cand = best1_len[v]
            else:
                cand = best2_len[v]
            val = 2 * d - cand
            if val > best:
                best = val
            v = u
            d += 1
        else:
            val = 2 * L - best1_len[v]
            if val > best:
                best = val

        out.append(str(L - best))

        v = 0
        for b in s:
            c = b - 97
            u = children[v].get(c)
            if u is None:
                u = len(children)
                children[v][c] = u
                children.append({})
                best1_len.append(INF)
                best1_src.append(-1)
                best2_len.append(INF)
                best2_src.append(-1)
            add_candidate(v, L, u)
            v = u
        add_candidate(v, L, -1)

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Check query when child missing at root: val = 2*0 - INF = -1e9, best0, break. ans L. Good.
Check query when child missing at node v: val uses best1_len[v], which could be INF if v=root no previous. Good.
Check query when child exists and best1_src[v] == u, cand=best2_len[v] maybe INF. val negative. Good.
Check final candidate after loop: val = 2*L - best1_len[v]. If best1_len[v] INF? If loop completed, v exists along path, so finite. But if L=0? no. Good.
Insertion: add_candidate before moving. For a string, root updated with source first child. Terminal updated. Good.

Potential issue: In query, if child exists and best1_src[v] != u, cand=best1_len[v]. But what if best1_len[v] is INF and best1_src[v] != u? Could happen if no strings in subtree(v) but child u exists? impossible. But if it happens, val negative. Good.
